Great Lakes Cannabis & Hemp Business Directory — Audited Final

Last audited: 30 October 2026. Ohio, Michigan, Indiana, Illinois, Wisconsin, Minnesota. Original file: Great Lakes Cannabis & Hemp Business Directory — Complete, Categories 1–12, dated 28 October 2026, 208 entries. This audit: separate fact-checking pass by an agent that did not write the original. Single self-contained HTML file; no external dependencies.

What this audit did — and what it did not do (read first)

Every one of the 208 original entries was checked against its own official website first, as the task required. Where a site could be opened, the verdict rests on that site. Where a site could not be opened, the entry is marked Unverified, not Removed, and the exact reason is stated — a failed load, a hijacked domain, or a homepage with no cannabis/hemp wording is not evidence that a firm closed. No phone number, email, street address, licence number or owner contact has been invented in this file; where the checker found none published on the business's own site, it reads “Not published”.

Rate-limit limitation — this affects many verdicts, and it must not be misread. During the audit, the page-fetch service repeatedly returned upstream 429 (rate-limit) errors, especially for Categories 1–3 and 4–6. Checkers correctly treated a 429 as a hard stop and did not retry, route around it, or substitute search snippets where the task forbade it — except for Category 2 remainder + Category 3, where the checker states plainly that its “Confirmed” verdicts rest on indexed copies of the firms' own sites, not a live direct open, and a direct re-check is still needed before publication. A 429 is a tool limit, not a site failure. That is why Categories 4, 5 and especially 6 are mostly Unverified: their prior original evidence is retained but was not re-confirmed in this pass.

Credentials limitation. No official registry lookup was run for every credential in this pass. Every bar, CPA, contractor, security, insurance-producer, hemp-licence or hemp-product licence/registration number in this file is therefore marked Unverified — lookup not checked, unless the wording is explicitly “as published on the firm's own site” (which is not a lookup confirmation). Numbers quoted as published on a firm's own site are: Matthews broker licence REC.2022007141 (OH, as published), Meta Commercial Cannabis brokerage Licence ID 40908061 (MN, as published), Anderson Realty MN licence 40402332 (as published), OG Cannabis Insurance MI licence #0111900 claimed on its own site (as published). None of those four has been confirmed against Ohio Division of Real Estate, Minnesota Department of Commerce, or any other regulator in this audit. Category 12 licence numbers are Not published / Verify across the board — the OISC/Purdue lists opened for Indiana (Halsema, HempRise, Mako) contain names and contacts but no licence numbers, and no other own site opened published a number. The one historic number carried from the original file, FLM Hemp Solutions IDIN-44794018, expired 31 December 2020; its current licence is Verify.

Checker-count discrepancies, corrected here by row count. Two checker summaries miscounted their own rows, and this file uses the row-by-row counts, not the summaries: (1) Categories 1–3: checker summary said Confirmed 30 / Unverified 32, but its 72 rows count to Confirmed 29 / Corrected 10 / Unverified 33 / Removed 0. (2) Categories 10–11: checker summary said Category 11 Confirmed 12 / Unverified 7, but its 26 Category 11 rows count to Confirmed 9 / Corrected 6 / Unverified 10 / Removed 1; combined Categories 10–11 are therefore Confirmed 11 / Corrected 10 / Unverified 11 / Removed 1, not Confirmed 14 / Unverified 8. Both discrepancies are flagged here rather than silently smoothed over.

How verdicts were applied. Confirmed = own-site (or, for Category 2 remainder + Category 3 only, indexed own-site) evidence supports the entry as listed, apart from credentials. Corrected = the business is kept, but a tag, Standard category, geography, contact, parent or scope in the original was wrong and is corrected in the rewritten report and in the audit row. Removed = concrete evidence the entry must not be carried forward (discontinued brand, or wrong-state listing with no office in the listed state). Unverified = kept in the rewritten report but flagged, because own-site evidence could not be obtained or did not name cannabis/hemp. Already listed = 0 in this audit — no entry was found, on the evidence checked, to already appear on a named attached South Central or national list; the original file's Cincinnati / Northern Kentucky flag is carried forward as a check, not as a finding (see Duplicates section). Hemp licence only and Licensed operators moved out are not verdicts in the audit table; they are separate lists below, and entries on those lists are removed from the rewritten main tables, as the task requires.

Verdict reconciliation — audit table sums exactly to the original total

Original total: 208. Audit verdicts: Confirmed 74 + Corrected 46 + Unverified 86 + Removed 2 + Already listed 0 = 208.

Rewritten main-table totals by category: 1 = 39, 2 = 21, 3 = 12, 4 = 13, 5 = 15, 6 = 14, 7 = 5, 8 = 3, 9 = 7, 10 = 7, 11 = 25 (26 minus CannaContent), 12 = 35 (46 minus Queen City Hemp, minus 9 hemp-licence-only, minus Crested River). Sum = 196. Category 7 keeps Leaf Trade in its section with a Software correction note (see below) so the count is preserved; it is not double-counted as a separate Software entry. Category 11 keeps PackTrack in its section with a Software correction note for the same reason.

Appendix — Original research notes, duplicate decisions, excluded operators and not-counted lists (carried forward, NOT re-audited)

The original file's sections after Category 12 — Duplicate Decisions, Excluded Operators (combined, both runs), Not-counted lists, Thin results, and Research Notes — are carried forward below verbatim from the original HTML, except where this audit supersedes a decision above (notably: Talaria/National Secure Transport counted once, Social Equity Solutions moved out of Michigan Category 10 in the original, and the duplicate/parent decisions in the Duplicates section above). Those original sections were not re-audited in this pass: their Verify flags, excluded-operator names and not-counted firms were not re-checked against live sites or registries here, and they must not be quoted as audited findings. They are included so no section of the original report is dropped.

Run 1 — Michigan

Excluded licensed operators / holders (not listed above)

No business listed above was identified as a Michigan CRA licensee or a tribal cannabis enterprise, and none was removed on that ground in this run. The following were encountered during research and are deliberately not listed, because they are (or are presented as) licensed operators, not ancillary service providers:

  • Licensed dispensaries/provisioning centres and retailers appearing in general security searches (e.g. Skymint Grand Rapids, House of Dank Traverse City, Dunegrass, Olswell, Fluresh, NOXX/Cookies Grand Rapids, Cloud Cannabis, Nature's ReLeaf, Crafted Leaf, Five Buds, High in the Pines, Interlochen Alternative Health) — CRA licensees, excluded by scope; no licence numbers are reproduced here.
  • Businesses whose listings bundle an operator licence with real estate (e.g. several Michigan Cannabis Properties and C3 CRE listings marked "license and real estate included") — the broker is listed; the licence holder/seller is not, and no licence number is attributed to the broker.
  • Cure8 (cure8.tech) — a cannabis IT/security/compliance provider with a dedicated Michigan service page naming Detroit, Grand Rapids, Warren, Sterling Heights, Ann Arbor, Lansing, Flint, Dearborn, Livonia, Troy, Westland, Farmington Hills, Kalamazoo, Wyoming, Rochester Hills, Southfield, Taylor, St. Clair Shores and Pontiac — not listed because no Michigan office address was verified on the page read; it is a "serving Michigan" candidate for follow-up, not a Michigan-office entry. If a Michigan office is verified, it belongs in Category 6 (and, for its compliance work, as a cross-reference to Category 10 in Run 2).
  • Surna Cultivation Technologies — documented Michigan project (24,000 SF indoor facility, Jackson, MI: mechanical engineering and equipment, 2-pipe fan coils and dehumidifiers) — not listed because no Michigan office was verified; it is a national equipment/engineering vendor serving Michigan, for follow-up under the national-companies rule.
  • General Michigan security firms found without cannabis-specific evidence (EPS Security, Traverse City and Grand Rapids; TKS Security; STT Security and Investigations; Brooks Security; Rockburn Security Agency; Centurion Security Solutions; Moores Security Services) — not listed; a general security licence alone is not evidence of serving cannabis/hemp clients. If a cannabis client page, CRA project or testimonial is found for any of them, they should be added to Category 6.

Run 1 — Indiana

Excluded licensed operators

None found to exclude in this run, for a structural reason that must be stated in the HTML notes: Indiana has no state-licensed cannabis cultivators, processors, dispensaries or testing labs to exclude, and no Minnesota/Illinois/Ohio/Michigan licensee operating an Indiana cannabis site was identified. The following were checked and NOT listed as directory entries (they are not "excluded licensees" — they are out-of-scope operator/retail types that must not be mistaken for Categories 1–6 businesses):

  • Indiana hemp retailers / CBD shops (e.g. Mojo's Hemp House & Dispensary, Indianapolis; Simple Garden Dispensary, Indianapolis; PharmFree Life, Indianapolis; Bourbon Country Cannabis, New Albany) — these are hemp-product retailers, not Categories 1–6 service businesses. They belong, if anywhere, to a Category 12 / retailer discussion in Run 2, not here. No licence number is published for them in this run and none is claimed.
  • Hemp growers/processors themselves (HempRise/Hemprise, Jeffersonville; BDX-Indiana/Biodynamic Ventures, Westfield; Halsema Hemp Farms, Clarks Hill) — these are operators, not service providers. They appear above only as the *clients/projects* that evidence the Category 5 contractors and the Category 4 landlord. Their hemp licences/registrations under the Office of Indiana State Chemist (OISC) were NOT verified in this run and must not be stated.
  • Tribal cannabis enterprises in Michigan, Minnesota and Wisconsin — none operating in Indiana was found; none is listed.
  • Out-of-state licensed cannabis operators with Michigan border dispensaries (e.g. New Buffalo, MI provisioning centres marketed to Indiana customers) — these are Michigan licensees, excluded by the commission scope, and are NOT counted as Indiana real-estate or service entries. Their 420 Property listings were used only to show the cross-border pattern, not as Indiana entries.

If a later check finds that any business listed above holds an Illinois, Michigan, Ohio or Minnesota cannabis licence (for example a multi-state operator's Indiana sales office), move it out of Categories 1–6 and record it here with the licence source — do not silently keep it in.

Thin results — summary

  • Wisconsin Cats 7–9 empty: no Wisconsin-office armoured-transport, waste, or staffing firm with cannabis/hemp-specific evidence was found (general cash firms with Wisconsin branches exist but none evidenced serving cannabis/hemp clients; non-compliant hemp destruction in Wisconsin is largely on-farm).
  • Michigan Cat 8 = 0 and Illinois Cat 8 = 0: genuinely thin — Illinois cannabis waste is served by Missouri-based or national firms with no verified Illinois office (Total Organics Recycling, Hazardous Waste Experts are serves-only, not counted).
  • Indiana Cat 9 = 0: no cannabis/hemp staffing firm with a verified Indiana office was found; national firms serve Indiana remotely only.
  • Michigan Cat 10 = 0 after the Social Equity Solutions move (see Duplicate Decisions). Kush Design Studio is counted in Cat 11, cross-referenced in Cat 10 only.
  • Illinois grow stores empty: Cat 11 has 6 marketing/design/packaging entries and 0 verified grow/hydroponics stores.
  • Armoured transport and waste-pickup gaps generally: only 5 Cat 7 and 3 Cat 8 entries region-wide; most states have at most one, and several of those carry Verify flags on branch-level evidence or office status.
  • Category 12 licence numbers unpublished across the board: no LPHE or MDA licence number for any Minnesota Cat 12 maker was published on its own site in Run 2; the same gap applies region-wide (Ohio Run 2: hemp licence/registration numbers were not published for any Cat 12 entry). The single published historic example is FLM Hemp Solutions IDIN-44794018 (expired 31 Dec 2020, current licence Verify).
  • Closed / merged / rebranded notes: Nemadji Manufacturing / Nemadji Processing (Osceola, WI) rebranded as United BioScience — NOT counted (whether hemp extraction continues under the new brand is unconfirmed; top Cat 12 follow-up). Lake Group / World Insurance merger, Morem & Waller / Sax Advisory Group, Bricks + Mortar domain/status, and other merger/rebrand flags are carried on their rows. Leaf Trade is now part of LeafLink (Illinois Cat 7, counted as a cash-management platform, not armoured transport).
  • Suggested follow-up sources for every gap (from the state files): OCM LPHE registry and MDA hemp lists (Minnesota Cat 12 licence numbers; also OCM lower-potency hemp edible product registrations); OISC lists (Indiana hemp licence-holder lists, including the March 2025 grower/handler contacts list); regulator vendor/licence lists generally; OCHBS (Ohio Cannabis Health & Business Summit) exhibitor lists and DCC build-out/contractor references (Ohio Cats 5, 9, 11); Minnesota follow-up candidates named in the files — Pott Farms; Surly, Fulton, Modist and Bent Paddle and other Minnesota brewers (Surly Take Five; Fulton THC sparkling water; Modist TINT; Bent Paddle CBD/THC seltzers; plus Minneapolis Cider/Trail Magic, 56 Brewing/Acre, Fair State/Inbound — each needs its own site confirmed before counting); Prairie Producers and Green Fox (industrial hemp fibre processors, AURI Hemp Market Spotlight — no addresses/phones/websites/current status confirmed); Nemadji/United BioScience (Osceola, WI); Turn It Up Media office location (Wisconsin Cat 11); and the regulator/vendor lists and exhibitor lists named in each state's Gaps section below.

Research Notes — gaps by state and by category (full, both runs)

run1 — Ohio

Gaps / thin areas and suggested follow-up sources

1. Category 3 (Insurance) is genuinely thin on confirmed Ohio evidence (2 entries). The Ohio market is dominated nationally by Cannasure (Cleveland HQ). Follow up: Ohio Department of Insurance producer lookup for cannabis-programme appointments; NCIA / National Cannabis Insurance Association member list; Ohio Cannabis Coalition partner/vendor lists; and direct checks of Marsh Columbus, Oswald Cleveland, Hylant (Toledo HQ) and World Insurance Associates for a cannabis page.

2. Category 4 (Real estate) is listing-led, not practice-led. No Ohio brokerage with a dedicated cannabis practice page was confirmed. Follow up: 420Property.com Ohio listings (broker-of-record per listing), LoopNet/CREXi Ohio cannabis-zoned searches, Ohio Realtors commercial directories, and the Zoned Properties / IIP Ohio portfolios (Ohio office to be verified first).

3. Category 5 (Construction) is the weakest on firm-site evidence. Only ETS has a firm-site cannabis offering; Claypool, Summit and Kiesland rest on a third-party directory classification. Follow up: Ohio DCC build-out/contractor references, Ohio Cannabis Health & Business Summit (OCHBS) exhibitor lists (ochbs.com), and permit records (e.g. warehouse-to-cultivation remodel permits) for named Ohio contractors; also check RBI CMS, Stockmeister Services, Danis (Cincinnati) and Messer Construction for Ohio cannabis projects.

4. Category 6 (Security) has no entry with a verified Ohio street address. Follow up: Ohio Department of Public Safety / Private Investigator Security Guard Services licence lookup for Ohio guard firms naming dispensary clients; OCHBS security exhibitors; and direct confirmation of a street address for 1st Security Services, Nationwide Security Service and Cure8's Ohio operations (or an Ohio-based installer).

5. Category 2 (Accounting) — named-CPA gap. Ohio Cannabis CPA and GreenGrowth CPAs have no named Ohio CPA or Ohio street address confirmed; MY DOPE CPA (Canton), InTrack CFO and Mindtrix (OCHBS exhibitors) were not opened. Follow up: Accountancy Board of Ohio licence lookup for the named principals; OCHBS exhibitor list; OSCPA member directory.

6. Category 1 (Law) — follow-ups not counted: Bricker Graydon Wyatt (Columbus, 100 S Third St, 614-227-2300) — confirm a cannabis practice page/attorney; KJK — confirm whether it acts for operators or only publishes employer/CBD commentary; Tucker Ellis, Mansour Gavin, Reminger, SSEG — confirm firm cannabis pages. Also check the Ohio State Bar Association and Columbus/Cleveland Metropolitan Bar Association cannabis-law sections/committees and the International Cannabis Bar Association Ohio members.

7. General sources not fully mined in this window: Ohio Cannabis Coalition (ohcann.com) member/partner lists; Ohio State University hemp extension partner lists; Ohio Department of Agriculture hemp programme lists (for hemp-only construction/insurance/accounting evidence); NCIA member directory filtered to Ohio; and the Ohio Cannabis Health & Business Summit exhibitor/speaker lists (which already yielded HBK and OCHBS-linked accountants).

8. Same-name traps checked and avoided: Columbus OH vs Columbus IN/GA/MS; Springfield OH (Pure Ohio Wellness, Springfield, Clark County, OH) vs Springfield IL/MO — no Springfield-office entry is claimed; Toledo OH vs Toledo (Spain) — Toledo appears only as a city served; Lansing MI vs Lansing IL, Portage MI vs Portage IN, and Duluth MN vs Duluth GA are not Ohio locations and no entry was sourced from them.

run1 — Michigan

Gaps and thin areas (Michigan, Categories 1–6)

  • Category 4 is thin at 3 entries. Michigan has a deep green-zoned market, but most cannabis property activity appears as individual-agent listings on 420 Property, LoopNet and MLS rather than as brokerages with a dedicated cannabis practice page. Follow-up: 420 Property Michigan broker/agent pages, LoopNet Michigan cannabis brokers, Keller Williams Domain (Victor Shauni, Galien/New Buffalo campus listing, licence 449095 in the listing — Verify whether this is a brokerage or an individual agent before adding), and county commercial boards.
  • Category 5 is thin at 3 entries, and only one is a trade contractor (SHC, Verify). Most Michigan cannabis build-out capacity sits inside general contractors, MEP firms and design-build firms that do not publish a cannabis page (e.g. the large Michigan contractors — Walbridge, Barton Malow, Christman, Granger, Rockford, Clark — were checked at headline level and no cannabis-specific practice evidence was found for them in this window; they are not padded in). Follow-up: Michigan CRA facility inspection / building-permit records, MJBizCon and Michigan CannaTech exhibitor lists, and the Michigan State University hemp extension / construction partner lists; also verify whether Emerald Business Park's general-contracting arm trades under a separate licensed entity name.
  • Category 6 is thin at 2 entries. Michigan's CRA security rules (camera coverage, 45-day retention, access control) mean many installers do CRA work without marketing it as "cannabis security." Follow-up: CRA-compliant installer referrals from Category 1 firms, Michigan security-guard agency licence rolls filtered for cannabis-client testimonials, and alarm companies that partner with IT installers (Grand Rapids Tech's unnamed burglar-alarm partner is one lead).
  • Category 3, Michigan-office verification: MFE Insurance and OG Cannabis Insurance are included on Michigan marketing pages only, with office/phone Verify flags. Kapnick, Ieuter and Spire are the three fully Michigan-office-verified insurance entries. Follow-up: Michigan Department of Insurance and Financial Services producer-licence lookup for each broker, and a direct check of MFE's claimed Michigan office.
  • Category 1, hemp-specific law: Only Oak Law expressly claims hemp/CBD work alongside cannabis; the large firms' pages are marijuana-focused. A hemp-only law entry (e.g. for Michigan hemp growers/processors under the state hemp plan) was not verified in this run — follow-up via the Michigan Hemp Coalition / state hemp licence lists and the State Bar Cannabis Law Section roster.
  • Category 2, licence numbers: No CPA licence number was published on any cannabis page read; all are marked Verify rather than filled from a licence database. Follow-up: Michigan Department of Licensing and Regulatory Affairs (LARA) CPA licence lookup for LEACIF, Yeo & Yeo, Maner Costerisan, Bair, Michigan Cannabis CPA and Manville & Schell principals.
  • Cross-state duplicate watch for assembly: Warner, Honigman, Butzel and Miller Johnson all serve Illinois in addition to Michigan (Honigman expressly; the others via multi-state practices) — check against the Illinois Run 1 file before finalising, and list each firm once with all states served. Spire is licensed in all six Great Lakes states — it will appear in every state file if the same rule is applied elsewhere; at assembly, list it once with all states served. C3 CRE's Tampa, FL office and MFE's national programme are out-of-region and are noted, not used as a listing basis.
  • Bricks + Mortar currency: The firm's current website could not be confirmed live in this window; its inclusion rests on a LoopNet profile and a 2020 sale report. Re-check the bricksmortarcannabis.com / bricksmortargroup.com domains and the firm's LoopNet presence before publication, and remove it if it is closed or merged.
  • No tribal cannabis enterprise is listed, per scope. No Michigan tribal enterprise was identified as a service provider (as opposed to an operator) in this run.

run2 — Indiana

Gaps / thin areas — read before using this file

1. Category 7 is thin (1 entry). Only Brink's/PAI has both a documented cannabis cash programme and an Indiana branch, and even there the branch-level cannabis service and the branch address/phone rest on listings, not on brinks.com itself. GardaWorld has the Indiana branches but no cannabis evidence; Loomis has neither an Indiana branch nor a cannabis programme on current evidence; Safe Harbor has the cannabis banking product but no Indiana office. Indiana's hemp businesses are mostly small and rural, and its (unlicensed) cannabis-adjacent retail is cash-heavy but served, if at all, by national programmes rather than Indiana-dedicated cash firms. Do not fill this category with general cash firms.

2. Category 8 is thin (1 entry) for a structural reason. Indiana has no licensed cannabis waste stream, and non-compliant hemp destruction is an on-farm regulatory event, not a commercial service: under the Indiana Hemp Plan (via USDA), hemp standing as a seed/grain crop found in violation of the acceptable THC level "shall be terminated in accordance with USDA's Remediation and Disposal Guidelines issued January 15, 2021"; the state seed commissioner gives written notice and an order to destroy within a commissioner-set timeframe, and the crop must be held and not moved without written approval. Indiana growers must also file a Post-Harvest, Termination and/or Destruction Report (per a secondary Indiana hemp guide). The Hoosier Hempster founders' 2020 crop burn (see 12.7) is a concrete example: destruction happened on the farm under state direction. That is why no Indiana commercial hemp-destruction firm was found, and why Stericycle affirmatively refuses medical-cannabis waste. Triumvirate is counted because it is a real cannabis-waste/EHS provider with an Indianapolis office — but its street address is still Verify, and its Indiana cannabis clients (as opposed to its national programme) are not separately evidenced.

3. Category 9 is empty (0 entries). This is a finding, not an omission: no cannabis/hemp staffing firm with an Indiana office was verified. National firms (Vangst, Hemp Temps, HempStaff and others) serve Indiana remotely or through job boards, and Indiana-office generalist staffing firms (CFS, SURGE) show no cannabis specialty. In a hemp-only state with a small grower base (AGDAILY 2022, quoting OISC's Don Robison: Indiana CBD growers fell from more than 400 to about 40 over three years), there is little local demand for a dedicated cannabis staffing office. A future check should look for Illinois/Chicago firms opening Indiana operations, not for more job boards.

4. Category 10 is thin (1 entry) and has a specific hole. J.S. Held is a genuine non-law-firm cannabis consultancy with a verified Indianapolis office, but it is forensic/regulatory consulting, not a licensing-application or social-equity consultancy — and Indiana has no cannabis licensing programme that would support one. No Indiana hemp-product compliance boutique (ATC manufacturer permits, QR-code labelling, child-resistant packaging rules, advertising restrictions, 21+ rules under SB 478) was verified; a secondary blog (atlrx.com) summarised 2025–2026 Indiana changes on those topics, but it is NOT a primary source and its details must not be cited as confirmed law without checking the statute/ATC pages. The Midwest Hemp Council is advocacy, not a consultancy. A future check should target firms advising hemp brands on ATC compliance specifically.

5. Category 11 is thin beyond its 4 entries (and 1 of the 4 is Verify). CODO (branding) and Jinko (packaging) are the two confirmed cannabis-specific entries; Maximum Grow is a general grow store with no cannabis wording on its homepage; Wine N Vine rests on directory evidence with its official site unopened. No Fort Wayne, Evansville, South Bend, Bloomington or Lafayette grow store was verified on its own site in this window (Harvest Moon Hydroponics, Indianapolis, 1234 N Capitol Ave, (317) 780-8070, appeared only in a directory listing and was not verified — follow-up, not counted). No Indiana packaging firm beyond Jinko and the unverified Berry Global lead was found serving hemp/CBD brands.

6. Category 12 is the strongest (8 entries) but carries the most Verify flags. 3Chi's address is a three-way conflict; Hemprise's official domain has lapsed into unrelated content and its latest confirmation is the March 2025 OISC list; Hoosier Hempster's official site was not opened and its street address is unpublished; FLM's current street address and current licence are Verify (only a 2020 licence number is on record); Mako and Hoosier Hempster are both counted on their own-brand limbs and would move to "not counted" if the assembler judges resale dominant. Two fibre/hempcrete leads (Dun Agro, Central Industrial Fiber) and one confectionery lead (Heartland Harvest) remain unverified and are the most valuable follow-ups for broadening Category 12 beyond CBD/THC brands into fibre, grain, textiles and hempcrete.

7. General Indiana caveat. Indiana's hemp sector contracted sharply after 2019–2020 (see Gap 3), several 2020–2022 licence-list entries are stale, and at least one major operator's domain (hemprise.com) has lapsed. Every Category 12 entry dated from a 2020–2022 source should be re-checked for current operation before the HTML is published, and the assembler should preserve the Verify flags rather than smoothing them over.

run2 — Illinois

Gaps / thin areas (explicit, not padded)

1. Category 7 — armoured transport: effectively empty for Illinois. Only one counted entry, and it is a payments platform, not an armoured carrier. No Illinois-office armoured firm with cannabis-specific evidence was found; national carriers' Illinois presence is documented only through general job listings. Assembler note: if the directory requires a true armoured carrier for Illinois, this category needs a fresh search (e.g., state transporter licensee lists, dispensary vendor disclosures) — do not promote Loomis/Brink's/GardaWorld on general cash evidence.

2. Category 8 — cannabis waste: zero Illinois-office entries. The firms actually serving Illinois are Missouri-based (Total Organics Recycling) or national (Hazardous Waste Experts). Stericycle (Bannockburn HQ) is the obvious follow-up but has no cannabis-specific programme verified. Non-compliant hemp destruction is grower-performed under Part 1200, not a marketed third-party service.

3. Category 9 — verification depth is weak. Only Lakeshore Talent has an own-site-confirmed Illinois office; Trovaro's site could not be opened (do not retry that open), Illinois Equity Staffing's domain currently returns unrelated content, and THC Staffing Group rests on secondary sources. Vangst is national with no verified Illinois office. Assembler may prefer a "Verify — not fully confirmed" sub-list for 9.2–9.4 rather than the main table.

4. Category 10 — thin on social-equity specialists. Konopé is the only firm with explicit social-equity application consulting verified on its own site, and it has no Illinois office. Illinois social-equity help is otherwise delivered by programmes/clinics (IDFPR technical-assistance list) and licensee-run programmes (GTI LEAP), not standalone consultancies with verified Illinois offices. Hemp-product compliance consulting as a standalone Illinois service was not found — Konopé's SOP work and ICS's packaging/labelling checks are the closest verified items.

5. Category 11 — grow/hydroponics retail: empty. No verified operating Illinois grow store was found (see 11 grow-store note). Marketing/design/packaging is solid (6 entries, 2 own-site confirmed).

6. Category 12 — licence numbers: none published. No hemp licence/registration number was found published for any counted Category 12 maker in the sources checked; all four are recorded as "Not published in sources checked" per the task rule, not invented. Illinois hemp food manufacturing beyond KifCure's contract work was not verified.

7. Same-name traps checked: No entry in this report relies on a Springfield, Bloomington or Lansing address; all counted Illinois addresses are Chicago-area, Maple Park, Arcola, Mundelein, Peoria, Itasca or West Chicago. Bloomington, IL appears only as a Loomis job-listing location (not listed).

8. Open-failure log: https://gotrovaro.com/ could not be opened (network/DNS/timeout, no HTTP status) — reported as a gap, not retried. https://www.illinoisequitystaffing.com/ opened but returned unrelated gambling/Samsung content — reported as a domain-status flag, with no contact copied from that page.

run2 — Wisconsin

Gaps, thin areas and follow-up sources

1. Categories 7, 8, 9 are empty by structure, not by oversight. With no licensed cannabis market, Wisconsin generates no licensed-operator cash-transport, regulated-waste or licensed-operator staffing demand. Hemp cash is largely banked/retail (Herring Bank's Wisconsin hemp programme is remote, no Wisconsin office); hemp waste destruction is on-farm; hemp hiring is direct/informal or via national platforms (Vangst, Mantis, Viridian, CannaGreenJobs) with no Wisconsin office. Do not pad these categories with general cash/waste/staffing firms or with Chicago/Minneapolis firms that have no Wisconsin office.

2. Category 10 is a single entry. Wisconsin hemp-product compliance work that is not law-firm work is thin and mostly advocacy-adjacent (Selthofner). Run 1 law firms carry most of the compliance load — cross-reference them. Follow-up: WIHFMA (Phillip Scott) and UW–Madison Extension hemp partners may surface a dedicated hemp-product compliance consultant; none was evidenced in this session.

3. Category 11 is thin and Verify-heavy. Only one grow/hydroponics store (Grow Big, Appleton) was confirmed at directory level; no second Wisconsin hydroponics store with cannabis/hemp evidence was found. Design entries rest on one portfolio page (Sam Hebel) and one 2019 article (Creative Cannamarketing); packaging rests on a 2020 product launch (Green Bay Packaging 1HEMP). Turn It Up Media is the most promising fifth entry if a Wisconsin office is verified.

4. Category 12 Verify flags to clear before publication: Driftless Extracts currency (2024 crawl); Hawkweed Hemp current site/operation; Green County Hemp phone conflict; Northwoods Molecular email; 3 Tall Pines phone/farm address; Crone Apothecary website/phone/email; TabEASE HQ vs retail address; Oliphant phone/email; BATCH address/phone/email against the official site; Heritage Hemp Farm farm address and email; all hemp licence/registration numbers (none published in sources checked).

5. Top follow-up candidates (not counted): Nemadji/United BioScience rebrand (Osceola) — confirm whether hemp extraction continues; Turn It Up Media office location; the retail-vs-maker check on the sponsor-list retailers named in Category 12 searched-not-counted (especially Sutherland CBD, Superior — cross-border Superior WI / Duluth MN trap — and Steve's Hemp, Eau Claire).

6. Same-name / cross-border traps applied: Madison WI (not Madison AL) throughout; Superior WI / Duluth MN not conflated (no Duluth firm counted as Wisconsin); Milwaukee/Chicago corridor firms counted only with a Wisconsin office (Loomis/Brink's branches noted but not counted for lack of cannabis evidence, not for lack of a Wisconsin office).

7. Regulatory overhang (context only, not a listing change): Secondary sources in this session describe a federal closing of the hemp-derived THC "loophole" with Wisconsin state lawmakers yet to pass a regulating bill, and a federal ban timetable in November (year per sources: 2026 coverage). If that timetable holds, several Category 12 hemp-THC makers' product lines (Delta-8, THCA, hemp-THC beverages/seltzers) may change status after the access date — the assembler should note the access date prominently and re-check commercially important entries before outreach, as with the Run 1/Run 2 caveat generally.

run2 — Minnesota

Gaps / thin areas and suggested follow-up sources

  • Cat 7 — thinnest category in Minnesota Run 2 (main = 1). No Minnesota armoured carrier with cannabis-client evidence was found. Follow-up: ask Minnesota dispensaries/OCM applicants directly which cash carriers they use; check Brink's, Loomis and GardaWorld Minnesota sales material (not just locations/jobs pages) for a cannabis sector page; check Minnesota credit unions/banks serving cannabis clients (e.g. via the Minnesota Credit Union Network) for their cash-transport partners; check OCM transporter licence data — noting that OCM-licensed cannabis transporters themselves are excluded, so only non-licence cash carriers count.
  • Cat 8 — thin (main = 1, equipment only). No Minnesota cannabis-waste pickup/destruction service and no named DEA-registered reverse distributor in Minnesota was found. Follow-up: MPCA hazardous-waste licensee lists for the seven metro counties (Anoka, Carver, Dakota, Hennepin, Ramsey, Scott, Washington) and MPCA for Greater Minnesota; DEA reverse-distributor registry filtered to Minnesota; OCM waste-plan filings in licence applications; Minnesota composting facilities approved under Minn. R. 7035.2836.
  • Cat 9 — thin (main = 1). Minnesota's staffing market is dominated by the single Edina firm (MCS) plus national platforms with no Minnesota office (Vangst). Follow-up: Minnesota DEED / OCM hiring-fair employer lists (OCM's 22 Oct 2026 Resource and Hiring Fair in Minneapolis, 30 open cannabis jobs); general Minnesota staffing firms (e.g. Twin Cities staffing agencies) with cannabis-client evidence — none was confirmed in this run and none has been listed without it.
  • Cat 10 — no dedicated social-equity-only consultant found. Social-equity help is bundled into general OCM licensing consulting (Oxendine, CS Consulting, Catalyst BC) and OCM's own free rolling verification (email ocm.licensing@state.mn.us). Follow-up: OCM Division of Community and Economic Development partner/grantee lists; Minnesota social-equity incubator/mentorship programme partners; University of Minnesota Extension cannabis business resources.
  • Cat 11 — marketing is the thin half. Grow stores are solid (Greener Gardens, EcoGarden Supply, both confirmed on their own sites). Marketing/design is thin: the only Minnesota-office packaging firm with cannabis evidence is John Roberts (podcast-evidenced, not a firm cannabis page), and the national cannabis-marketing agencies serving Minnesota (Hood Collective, Cannabis Marketing Agency) have no Minnesota office verified. Follow-up: Minnesota printers/packagers beyond John Roberts (check Minnesota Printing Industry / trade-show exhibitor lists); Minneapolis/St. Paul branding agencies with dispensary clients; packaging suppliers via OCM applicant vendor lists.
  • Cat 12 — deepest category, but licence numbers are the gap. Nine main-table makers are evidenced, yet NO LPHE or MDA licence number for any of them was published on its own site in this run. Follow-up (essential before publication if numbers are required): OCM LPHE manufacturer/wholesaler registry; MDA current hemp programme licence-holder list (the list found in this run is an older mdawebdev mirror and gives licence *types* without numbers); OCM lower-potency hemp edible product registrations. Also verify: Stigma's correct municipality; Utepils's current THC dose (5 mg vs 10 mg page inconsistency); Superior Molecular's current trading name; Hemp Acres' and Minnesota Hemp Farms' 2026 operating status and live domains; the seven additional THC-beverage makers listed under Cat 12 (Surly, Fulton, Modist, Bent Paddle, Minneapolis Cider/Trail Magic, 56 Brewing/Acre, Fair State/Inbound) — each needs its own site confirmed before counting.
  • Industrial hemp (non-cannabinoid) is thinner than the beverage boom suggests. The strongest non-cannabinoid programme (Lower Sioux hempcrete) is tribal and excluded; the non-tribal fibre/food processors found are Hemp Acres, Minnesota Hemp Farms, Prairie Producers and Green Fox, of which only the first two are main-table entries and both are Verify/secondary-evidenced. Follow-up: AURI hemp market reports and buyer maps; University of Minnesota hemp extension partner lists; MDA hemp processor lists filtered to fibre/grain (not cannabinoid) processors; Winona's Hemp & Heritage Farm, Osage, MN (Winona LaDuke) — hemp plan page found, but no finished-product brand confirmed, so it is a follow-up candidate only, not a main-table entry.
  • Federal-date risk note for Cat 12 (not a Minnesota finding): the federal hemp-definition change to 0.4 mg total THC/container scheduled for 11 Dec 2026 would, if it takes effect as written, remove most Minnesota LPHE beverages/edibles from the hemp lane. The makers above are listed as Minnesota hemp makers as of 28 Oct 2026; their status after that date must be re-checked and is not asserted here.

End of directory. Sources: Run 1 and Run 2 state files listed in Method & scope. Date accessed for all entries: 28 October 2026. Verify flags must be re-checked before outreach or publication.
